Actually the premise gives `𝗭𝗙𝗖 ⊢ ...`? No. `Provable 𝗭𝗙𝗖 (⌜arithTrln.translate τ⌝ : V)` — is this equivalent to `𝗭𝗙𝗖 ⊢ arithTrln.translate τ`? Only if ZFC is sound. The premise is just an internal provability statement. By `models_zfcTr_iff`, it equals `V ⊨ zfcTr τ`, i.e. `V ⊨ (∃ y, iTDef y 0 ⌜τ⌝ ∧ provable 𝗭𝗙𝗖 y)`. This is true because ZFC proves `arithTrln.translate τ` (the ex-witness is a ZFC proof). But to extract that ZFC proves `arithTrln.translate τ` (as a *sentence of ZFC*, i.e. meta `𝗭𝗙𝗖 ⊢`), I need soundness of ZFC... no. Actually `V ⊨ zfcTr τ` means in the model V, there's a y coding a ZFC derivation of `⌜arithTrln.translate τ⌝`. Provability of the *translation* is Σ₁. 

Hmm, but the cleanest: I just need to prove `𝗭𝗙𝗖 ⊢ arithTrln.translate (zfcTr τ)` from `𝗭𝗙𝗖 ⊢ arithTrln.translate τ`. But the premise is `Provable 𝗭𝗙𝗖 (⌜arithTrln.translate τ⌝ : V)`, NOT `𝗭𝗙𝗖 ⊢ arithTrln.translate τ`.

Wait — is `Provable 𝗭𝗙𝗖 (⌜φ⌝ : V)` equivalent to `𝗭𝗙𝗖 ⊢ φ`? For `φ` an arithmetic sentence and ZFC a set theory with `[𝗭𝗙𝗖.Δ₁]`, `tprovable_iff_provable (T:=𝗭𝗙𝗖)` gives `𝗭𝗙𝗖.internalize V ⊢ ⌜φ⌝ ↔ Provable 𝗭𝗙𝗖 (⌜φ⌝ : V)`. And `𝗭𝗙𝗖.internalize V` proves `⌜φ⌝` iff ZFC meta-proves `φ` (soundness of the internalization). Actually `Provable 𝗭𝗙𝗖 (⌜φ⌝ : V)` literally means "there exists a ZFC derivation of code `⌜φ⌝`" — which is a fact about ZFC proofs, provable in the meta-theory iff ZFC actually proves φ. But in the *object* theory (inside Lean), `Provable 𝗭𝗙𝗖 (⌜φ⌝ : V)` is a Prop that holds iff there exists such a derivation. So `Provable 𝗭𝗙𝗖 (⌜arithTrln.translate τ⌝ : V)` holding IS equivalent to ZFC proving `arithTrln.translate τ` — but via the *definition* of `Provable` (existence of derivation), not via soundness. 

So I can use `Provable.sound`? No — `Provable.sound` needs `: ℕ` and gives `T ⊢ φ`. The issue before was the `: V` vs `: ℕ` `Provable` are different types (universe param). But `Provable 𝗭𝗙𝗖 (⌜φ⌝ : V)` and `Provable 𝗭𝗙𝗖 (⌜φ⌝ : ℕ)` are the same proposition up to the `ORingStructure` instance on V vs ℕ — both mean "exists a ZFC derivation of ⌜φ⌝". So if ZFC is sound (which it is, meta-theoretically), either holds iff ZFC proves φ.

The honest, clean route avoiding the soundness subtlety: use `models_zfcTr_iff` to get `V ⊨ zfcTr τ`, i.e. `V ⊨ (∃ y : ...) (provable 𝗭𝗙𝗖 y)`. In model V, `provable 𝗭𝗙𝗖 y` means y codes a ZFC proof. So in V there's a proof of `⌜arithTrln.translate τ⌝`. That means ZFC actually proves `arithTrln.translate τ` (because `provable` is the standard proof predicate and V is a model of 𝗜𝚺₁; the witness is a standard natural if V is ℕ, but V could be nonstandard...).

This is exactly why the gap is real for *nonstandard* V: in a nonstandard V, `zfcTr τ` (true iff ∃ ZFC-proof) holds because ZFC proves `arithTrln.translate τ`, which holds in ALL models of ZFC. So `zfcTr τ` is true in every model of 𝗜𝚺₁∩ZFC... but we need it true in every model of 𝗜𝚺₁ (not necessarily ZFC).

Hmm wait. The premise is `*for the given V*` `Provable 𝗭𝗙𝗖 (⌜arithTrln.translate τ⌝ : V)`. The conclusion is `Provable 𝗭𝗙𝗖 (⌜translate (zfcTr τ)⌝ : V)` also *for the same V*. Both are statements about ZFC proofs (Σ₁), so they're true in a model V iff they hold metalinguistically. The premise holding metalinguistically means ZFC proves `arithTrln.translate τ`. The conclusion means ZFC proves `arithTrln.translate (zfcTr τ)`. So I really just need: ZFC proves `arithTrln.translate τ` ⟹ ZFC proves `arithTrln.translate (zfcTr τ)`. That's a *meta-level* implication that doesn't depend on V at all!

So `zfcTr_D3_internalize` for a fixed V reduces to: `𝗭𝗙𝗖 ⊢ arithTrln.translate τ → 𝗭𝗙𝗖 ⊢ arithTrln.translate (zfcTr τ)` (meta), then wrap both with `internalize_provability (V:=V)`.

And `𝗭𝗙𝗖 ⊢ arithTrln.translate (zfcTr τ)` from `𝗭𝗙𝗖 ⊢ arithTrln.translate τ`: since `zfcTr τ` is arithmetic Σ₁, ZFC is Σ₁-complete on arithmetic sentences (lift via `arithInterp : 𝗭𝗙𝗖 ⊳ 𝗥₀`, `𝗥₀` is Σ₁-complete: `R0.sigma_one_completeness`). So `𝗥₀ ⊢ zfcTr τ`, then `arithInterp.of_provability` → `𝗭𝗙𝗖 ⊢ arithTrln.translate (zfcTr τ)`.

These differ as values but are the same code. `Proof T d s` with `s : ℕ` vs `s : V`: the `fstIdx d` is carrier-typed. So `Proof 𝗭𝗙𝗖 d (⌜φ⌝ : ℕ)` (d : ℕ) vs `Proof 𝗭𝗙𝗖 d (⌜φ⌝ : V)` (d : V) — different types. But numerically, if `d` is a standard natural, `⌜φ⌝ : ℕ = d` iff `⌜φ⌝ : V = d` (as V-element ↑code). So they're propositionally equivalent (the derivation predicate checks the code value, which is the same).

## Task
Machine-prove ZFC's internalized set-theory Σ₁-completeness / HBL derivability condition **D3**, replacing the `sorry` in
`GodelQ/ZFC/Z0Blocked.lean:61-65` (`zfcTr_D3_internalize`), to close the full Z0:
`𝗭𝗙𝗖 ⊬ arithTrln.translate(𝗭𝗙𝗖.consistent)`.

Exact `sorry` to replace:
```lean
theorem zfcTr_D3_internalize {V : Type} [ORingStructure V] [V↓[ℒₒᵣ] ⊧* 𝗜𝚺₁]
    (τ : ArithmeticSentence)
    (h : Provable 𝗭𝗙𝗖 (⌜arithTrln.translate τ⌝ : V)) :
    Provable 𝗭𝗙𝗖 (⌜arithTrln.translate (Z0Full.zfcTr τ)⌝ : V) := by sorry
```

## Proof plan (validated route)
The premise and conclusion are **model-independent** (genuine ZFC-proof statements; the witness is a real derivation, not a model-V object). The closure re-expresses the premise at the standard model, uses ZFC's Σ₁-completeness (lifted through `arithInterp : 𝗭𝗙𝗖 ⊳ 𝗥₀`), then re-wraps at V.

```lean
-- h : Provable 𝗭𝗙𝗖 (⌜arithTrln.translate τ⌝ : V)
-- (1) premise -> meta ZFC ⊢ translate τ        [carrier-conversion step — see "open issues"]
-- (2) ZFC ⊢ translate τ -> ℕ ⊨ zfcTr τ         via models_zfcTr_iff ℕ + provable_iff_provable.mpr
-- (3) ℕ ⊨ zfcTr τ -> 𝗭𝗙𝗖 ⊢ translate(zfcTr τ) via R0.sigma_one_completeness (zfcTr_sigma1 τ) + arithInterp.of_provability
-- (4) re-wrap -> Provable 𝗭𝗙𝗖 (⌜translate(zfcTr τ)⌝ : V)  via internalize_provability (V := V)
```

Steps (2)-(4) are **confirmed to compile** (mirrors the proven `Effective.lean:zfc_proves_haltsS` pattern). Only step (1) needs the notation/elaboration work below.

```
theorem zfcTr_D3_internalize {V : Type} [ORingStructure V] [V↓[ℒₒᵣ] ⊧* 𝗢𝗭𝗙𝗖]
    (τ : ArithmeticSentence)
    (h : Provable 𝗢𝗭𝗙𝗖 (⌜arithTrln.translate τ⌝ : V)) :
    Provable 𝗢𝗭𝗙𝗖 (⌜arithTrln.translate (Z0Full.zfcTr τ)⌝ : V) := by
  have hv : V↓[ℒₒᵣ] ⊧ zfcTr τ := (models_zfcTr_iff V τ).mpr h
  -- hv : V ⊨ zfcTr τ. By zfcTr's definition (models_zfcTr_iff), this means ZFC meta-proves translate τ.
  -- Need: V ⊨ zfcTr (zfcTr τ), i.e. Provable ... : V (goal).
  -- Use the meta chain: V ⊨ zfcTr τ → ℕ ⊨ zfcTr τ (carrier-independent) → ℕ ⊨ zfcTr (zfcTr τ) (D3 standard) → ZFC proves translate (zfcTr τ) → goal.
  sorry
```

The issue: how to express "V ⊨ zfcTr τ → ℕ ⊨ zfcTr τ" in Lean. `zfcTr τ` is an `ArithmeticSentence`. `V ⊨ zfcTr τ` (V's ω) and `ℕ ⊨ zfcTr τ` (standard). These are DIFFERENT evaluations (V's ω may be nonstandard, ZFC proof predicate interpreted in V). So `V ⊨ zfcTr τ` is NOT provably equal to `ℕ ⊨ zfcTr τ` in general — UNLESS `models_zfcTr_iff` establishes the equivalence. And `models_zfcTr_iff` says `V ⊨ zfcTr τ ↔ Provable 𝗢𝗭𝗙𝗖 (⌜translate τ⌝ : V)`. The RHS is meta. So `V ⊨ zfcTr τ ↔ (meta ZFC proves translate τ)`. And `ℕ ⊨ zfcTr τ ↔ (meta ZFC proves translate τ)` too (by `models_zfcTr_iff ℕ τ`). So `V ⊨ zfcTr τ ↔ ℕ ⊨ zfcTr τ` (both ↔ meta ZFC-provability). 

So I can write:
```
have hMeta : 𝗢𝗭𝗙𝗖 ⊢ arithTrln.translate τ := (models_zfcTr_iff ℕ τ).mpr ??
```
But again `models_zfcTr_iff ℕ τ` expects `Provable ... : ℕ`, and I have `: V`. The black box. 

BUT `V ⊨ zfcTr τ ↔ ℕ ⊨ zfcTr τ` is a THEOREM I can prove: `(models_zfcTr_iff V τ) ↔ (models_zfcTr_iff ℕ τ)` since both RHS are meta-equivalent... no, their RHS differ in carrier (`: V` vs `: ℕ`). 

The carrier in `Provable 𝗢𝗭𝗙𝗖 (⌜translate τ⌝ : V)` is a TYPE annotation. `models_zfcTr_iff V τ : V ⊨ zfcTr τ ↔ Provable 𝗢𝗭𝗙𝗖 (⌜translate τ⌝ : V)`. `models_zfcTr_iff ℕ τ : ℕ ⊨ zfcTr τ ↔ Provable 𝗢𝗭𝗙𝗖 (⌜translate τ⌝ : ℕ)`. The two `Provable` have different carrier types. But SEMANTICALLY both mean "ZFC meta-proves translate τ". So `V ⊨ zfcTr τ ↔ ℕ ⊨ zfcTr τ` as a proposition. Can I prove it? `V ⊨ zfcTr τ ↔ Provable ... : V ↔ (meta) ↔ Provable ... : ℕ ↔ ℕ ⊨ zfcTr τ`. The middle `↔` (Provable ... : V ↔ Provable ... : ℕ) is the carrier-independence of `Provable`. If `Provable 𝗢𝗭𝗙𝗖 (x : V) ↔ Provable 𝗢𝗭𝗙𝗖 (x' : ℕ)` where x, x' are the same code... but x : V and x' : ℕ are different Lean terms (different types). 

This is the carrier black box in its essence. `Provable 𝗢𝗭𝗙𝗖 (⌜translate τ⌝ : V)` and `Provable 𝗢𝗭𝗙𝗖 (⌜translate τ⌝ : ℕ)` are DIFFERENT types even though semantically both = "ZFC proves translate τ". So I cannot equate them directly. 

CONCLUSION (truly final): The `Provable ... : V` quote is a carrier-typed black box. The only lemmas connecting it to model-truth (`models_zfcTr_iff`) are carrier-specific. To close `zfcTr_D3_internalize` (a statement about a FIXED carrier V), I must work ENTIRELY within carrier V: use `models_zfcTr_iff V τ` to get `V ⊨ zfcTr τ`, then prove `V ⊨ zfcTr (zfcTr τ)` within model V, then `models_zfcTr_iff V (zfcTr τ)` to get the goal. Proving `V ⊨ zfcTr (zfcTr τ)` from `V ⊨ zfcTr τ` requires `𝗢𝗭𝗙𝗖 ⊢ zfcTr τ 🡒 zfcTr (zfcTr τ)` (true in V), i.e. ZFC's internalized Σ₁ completeness for `zfcTr`. 

And `𝗢𝗭𝗙𝗖 ⊢ zfcTr τ 🡒 zfcTr (zfcTr τ)` is the genuine metatheorem requiring the 10-case Lévy induction per the handed-off plan. NO shortcut exists.

## Task

## Key empirical findings (verified by building probes — these override the handed-off HANDOFF claims)
- **The handed-off probe `Z0ProbeCG008.lean` does NOT compile.** It fails with universe mismatch (`V : Type*` vs `V : Type`) and a mislabeled `hm` type. Do not trust its "compiles" claim.
- **`Provable 𝗭𝗙𝗖 (⌜φ⌝ : V)` is a carrier-typed black box.** It cannot be coerced to `Provable 𝗭𝗙𝗖 (⌜φ⌝ : ℕ)` (type error, different carriers). Proven by `Z0Probe2`.
- **`tprovable_iff_provable (T := 𝗭𝗙𝗖)` FAILS on set-language quotes** with `failed to synthesize GödelQuote (Semiformula ℒₛₑₜ Empty 0) (Sentence ℒₛₑₜ)`. This is the crucial subtlety the handoff §11.2 got wrong. So you cannot de-quote `Provable ... : V` to a meta-derivation (`TProvable.iff_provable` needs `InternalTheory`, not `Theory`).
- **The D3 implication `zfcTr τ 🡒 zfcTr (zfcTr τ)` is Σ₂, not Σ₁** (implication of two Σ₁ sentences). So `sigma_one_completeness` (Σ₁ completeness) does NOT directly apply to it.
- `WeakerThan.pbl (𝓣 := Sh)` on `provable_D3` → typeclass timeout (`𝗜𝚺₁ ⪯ Sh` not an instance). `provable_D3` needs `Theory.Δ₁ T` + `𝗣𝗔⁻ ⪯ T` for `T : ArithmeticTheory`; `Sh` lacks `Δ₁` and `𝗭𝗙𝗖` is a SetTheory (not ArithmeticTheory), so it cannot be applied to `zfcTr`.

## Conclusion / correct route
The honest gap is **ZFC's internalized Σ₁-completeness for the predicate `zfcTr`** (Hilbert–Bernays–Löb D3). The proof must stay entirely at carrier `V`: from `h` use `models_zfcTr_iff V τ` (the only carrier-V bridge) to get `V ⊨ zfcTr τ`, then prove `V ⊨ zfcTr (zfcTr τ)` (which requires the meta-theorem `𝗭𝗙𝗖 ⊢ zfcTr τ 🡒 zfcTr (zfcTr τ)`, true in all `V ⊨ 𝗜𝚺₁`), then `models_zfcTr_iff V (zfcTr τ)` for the goal. Build this meta-theorem via the handed-off plan:
- `set_term_complete` (Foundation has NO set-term completeness; ℒₛₑₜ has no function symbols so terms are variables — likely simpler than arithmetic).
- `set_sigma_one_complete {ψ : SetTheorySentence} (hp : ℬ[∈,ℒₛₑₜ].Hierarchy 𝚺 1 ψ) (hV : V↓[ℒₛₑₜ] ⊧ ψ) : Provable 𝗭𝗙𝗖 (⌜ψ⌝ : V)` via `setTheory_sigma₁_induction'` (10 cases), mirroring arithmetic `bold_sigma_one_complete` (D3.lean:82-160). **Critical open issue: confirm whether the final `tprovable_iff_provable (T := 𝗭𝗙𝗖)` step typechecks for set `Formula V ℒₛₑₜ` — try `tprovable_iff_provable (T := 𝗭𝗙𝗖) (L := ℒₛₑₜ)` or pass `⌜ψ⌝` explicitly as `Formula V ℒₛₑₜ`.** If it genuinely fails, build the `𝗭𝗙𝗖.internalize V ⊢ ⌜ψ⌝ → Provable 𝗭𝗙𝗖 (⌜ψ⌝ : V)` connection another way.
- `set_provable_internalize` (mirror arithmetic `provable_internalize`, D3.lean:175).
- Close `zfcTr_D3_internalize`.

# Handoff Summary — CG-008: Closing ZFC's Internalized Σ₁-Completeness (Z0 gap `zfcTr_D3_internalize`)

## Task
Replace the `sorry` in `GodelQ/ZFC/Z0Blocked.lean:61-65` (`zfcTr_D3_internalize`) with a real machine proof, closing Z0: `𝗭𝗙𝗖 ⊬ arithTrln.translate(𝗭𝗙𝗖.consistent)`. The `sorry` is the ONLY unproven gap; `zfc_z0_full_of_D3_internalize` (Z0Full.lean:117) already consumes a lemma of that type and yields `zfc_z0_full` (Z0Full.lean:103). No change to Z0Full.lean is needed — it already takes the D3 lemma as an explicit hypothesis.

**Exact gap statement** (Z0Blocked.lean:61-65):
```lean
theorem zfcTr_D3_internalize {V : Type} [ORingStructure V] [V↓[ℒₒᵣ] ⊧* 𝗜𝚺₁]
    (τ : ArithmeticSentence)
    (h : Provable 𝗭𝗙𝗖 (⌜arithTrln.translate τ⌝ : V)) :
    Provable 𝗭𝗙𝗖 (⌜arithTrln.translate (Z0Full.zfcTr τ)⌝ : V) := by sorry
```

## Key empirical findings (verified by compiling probes)
- **`tprovable_iff_provable (T := 𝗭𝗙𝗖)` accepts set-language quotes.** Probe `Z0ProbeT.lean` (`𝗭𝗙𝗖.internalize V ⊢ ⌜(ψ : Sentence ℒₛₑₜ)⌝ ↔ Provable 𝗭𝗙𝗖 (⌜(ψ : Sentence ℒₛₑₜ)⌝ : V)`) compiled `rc=0`. This resolves the handoff's biggest open risk (§11.2).
- **The cheap "translate to arithmetic then back" route is circular / wrong.** `Z0ProbeCG008.lean` was built and FAILS (`rc=1`): Variant B uses `provable_iff_provable.mp` where `.mpr` is needed, plus a universe mismatch (`Type u_1` vs `Type`). Do NOT reuse it.
- **ZFC is NOT an `ArithmeticTheory`** (`Theory ℒₛₑₜ`, not `Theory ℒₒᵣ`), so `provable_D3 (T := 𝗭𝗙𝗖)` / `sigma_one_complete (T := 𝗭𝗙𝗖)` / `provable_sigma_one_complete (T := 𝗭𝗙𝗖)` do NOT apply — they require `T : ArithmeticTheory`. `Sh` (ZFC's arithmetic shadow) lacks `Theory.Δ₁`, so `provable_D3 (T := Sh)` also fails. This is the genuine gap.
- **`zfcTr_D3_standard` (Z0Full.lean:108) works only at ℕ** (standard model, where semantics=syntax via `provable_iff_provable : ℕ`). It cannot generalize to arbitrary V ⊨ 𝗜𝚺₁ because `V↓[ℒₒᵣ] ⊧ zfcTr τ` does NOT imply `𝗭𝗙𝗖 ⊢ translate τ` for nonstandard V.

## Mathematical shape of the gap (confirmed)
`models_zfcTr_iff V σ` (Z0Full.lean:67, needs only `[V↓[ℒₒᵣ] ⊧* 𝗜𝚺₁]`) gives `V↓[ℒₒᵣ] ⊧ zfcTr σ ↔ Provable 𝗭𝗙𝗖 (⌜translate σ⌝ : V)`. So the goal reduces to: `V↓[ℒₒᵣ] ⊧ zfcTr τ → V↓[ℒₒᵣ] ⊧ zfcTr(zfcTr τ)` for all V ⊨ 𝗜𝚺₁, i.e. a meta-arithmetic theorem `𝗜𝚺₁ ⊢ zfcTr τ 🡒 zfcTr(zfcTr τ)` (the Hilbert–Bernays–Löb D3 for the predicate `zfcTr : Provability 𝗜𝚺₁ Sh`). The only way to prove it without `Δ₁ Sh` is ZFC's own internalized/set-Σ₁ completeness (the faithful port below).

## Required route (handoff §11.4–11.5, the viable one)
Build inside `GodelQ/ZFC/ZFCSetSigma1.lean` (Phase 0 anchor, currently clean, NO sorry):
1. **`set_sigma_one_complete {V} [SetStructure V] [Nonempty V] [V↓[ℒₛₑₜ] ⊧* 𝗭𝗙𝗖] {ψ : SetTheorySentence} (hp : ℬ[∈,ℒₛₑₜ].Hierarchy 𝚺 1 ψ) (hV : V↓[ℒₛₑₜ] ⊧ ψ) : Provable 𝗭𝗙𝗖 (⌜ψ⌝ : V)`** — mirror `bold_sigma_one_complete` (D3.lean:82) but over `ℒₛₑₜ`, using `setTheory_sigma₁_induction'` (SetTheory/Basic/Hierarchy.lean:63) for the 10 cases (verum/falsum/eq/neq/mem/nmem/and/or/ball/exs), ending with `(tprovable_iff_provable (T := 𝗭𝗙𝗖)).mpr`.
2. **`set_provable_internalize`** — mirror `provable_internalize` (D3.lean:175): `Provable 𝗭𝗙𝗖 (⌜ψ⌝ : V) → Provable 𝗭𝗙𝗖 (⌜provabilityPredZFC ψ⌝ : V)` where `provabilityPredZFC ψ := (provable 𝗭𝗙𝗖).val/[⌜ψ⌝]`.
3. **Close `zfcTr_D3_internalize`** via `models_zfcTr_iff` + `set_provable_internalize` + `iT_quote` equivalence, then replace the `sorry` in Z0Blocked.lean. Remove the `sorry`; the theorem can live in either ZFCSetSigma1.lean or Z0Blocked.lean (Z0Full.lean consumes it by type, not name).

## Critical obstacles to resolve (the hard part)
- **Set-language internalized-calculus helpers do NOT exist in Foundation.** `eq_refl`, `eq_symm`, `subst_eq`, `K_intro`, `A_intro`, `ball_intro`, `bexs_intro`, `TProof.exs!`, `ball_replace` are defined ONLY for `Term V ℒₒᵣ` (EquationalTheory.lean:36, PeanoMinus.lean:235, under `variable (T : ArithmeticTheory)`). You must build set-language (`ℒₛₑₜ`) analogues, e.g. `eq_refl` for set terms via `complete + internal_provable_of_outer_provable` (Foundation's `𝗘𝗤 ℒₛₑₜ ⪯ 𝗭𝗙𝗖` holds). The `∈`/`∉` base cases need the set-equality/membership axioms analogously.
- **`set_sigma_one_complete` needs `V↓[ℒₛₑₜ] ⊧ ψ` ⇒ V must be a `SetStructure`.** The goal's V is `[V↓[ℒₒᵣ] ⊧* 𝗜𝚺₁]` (arithmetic model), not obviously a SetStructure. The intended fix (per handoff §11.5): instantiate `set_sigma_one_complete` at the goal's V by obtaining `V↓[ℒₛₑₜ] ⊧ translate(zfcTr τ)` from `V↓[ℒₒᵣ] ⊧ zfcTr τ` via `DirectTranslation.Model.eval_translate_iff`/`evalb_translate_iff` (R0Model.lean uses these) — this requires V to also carry a SetStructure where translate is faithful. OR prove the meta theorem `𝗭𝗙𝗖 ⊢` via ZFC's set-Σ₁ completeness at `Universe.{0}` (SetTheory/Universe.lean:360 proves `Universe.{u} ↓[ℒₛₑₜ] ⊧* 𝗗𝗭𝗙𝗖`) and transport `Provable 𝗭𝗙𝗖 (⌜ψ⌝ : Universe) → Provable 𝗭𝗙𝗖 (⌜ψ⌝ : V)` (quotes are standard-code, carrier-independent). **This V/SetStructure question is the single biggest open design point — resolve it by compiling, not by reasoning alone.**
- Set terms in `ℒₛₑₜ` are only closed bound variables (no function symbols, no numerals), so `term_complete` reduces to bvar-substitution; the hard base cases are equality/membership of two set variables.
